$12,000 Buffalo–New York Fares Making Headlines—I’m Finding Nonstops For $337 Roundtrip

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A $12,203 airfare between Buffalo and New York about 300 miles each way – is going viral as evidence of how expensive it’s gotten to fly home for the holidays. The TikTok video that started it off though shows a roundtrip price (not one-way) and connecting both directions in Florida (not non-stop). Meanwhile, nonstops on that route seem to start at $337 roundtrip. Kristen Mirand says she was trying to get home to Buffalo and received a notification for a $200 flight from New York. Then she looked at returning, and found five-figure prices with connections in Orlando and Fort Lauderdale. She joked that she’d start walking. The New York Post ran with “Flight from Buffalo to NYC reportedly reached $12K — as holiday airfare hits an all-time high” and suggests higher fuel costs and holiday demand are involved here. ZeroHedge describes a “roughly 90 minute flight,” and launches into airlines keeping fares high after fuel prices fall. It ends with the prospect of airlines “laughing all the way to the bank.” Look at the flights actually displayed in the video: $11,400 roundtrip, returning through Orlando. These are 7 and 9 hour trips from Buffalo to JFK. $12,203 roundtrip, returning through Fort Lauderdale. These are 7 and 8.5 hour trips. Every price is labeled round trip. you’re seeing the cost of the whole ticket when you select a return. This wasn’t a $12,000 nonstop and none of these itineraries gets you there in 90 minutes. I’m seeing nonstop roundtrips around $337 on Delta Air Lines, JetBlue, United Airlines and American Airlines, checking across the New York airports. Even if you want to connect in Florida, Frontier Airlines can be as low as $210 roundtrip. She doesn’t show her dates, but at those prices be flexible! My guess is that the pricing is breaking somewhere as the system puts together this unnatural itinerary. A fare between Buffalo and New York comes with rules about how you can travel and Florida connections aren’t usually included in the routing rules. A search can wind up combining separate fares for different pieces of the trip instead of using the simple fare between the two cities. Bad fare combining or an error putting together the outbound and return can cause weird outcomes. While the last seats on a flight can be expensive, “last seat available” doesn’t seem like the explanation for a $12,000 Buffalo–New York price. Airfares are up. Hopper’s says Thanksgiving domestic roundtrips average $402, up 31%, and Christmas $452, up 23%. With millions of fare quotes being generated, even a tiny error rate is going to produce outlier results. Mistake fares can be fares that are too high, not just too low. Faced with a fare like this, I’d: Search for nonstops on the date I actually want. Start with the trip I’d choose to take before considering a seven-hour detour. Check JFK, LaGuardia and Newark. Restricting the search to one airport leaves out alternatives that may work just as well. Search the airline websites directly. See whether the airline produces the same price, rather than treating one Google Flights result as the answer. Start a fresh search and compare one-ways with the roundtrip. Check the dates, cabin, passenger count and currency. Separate searches can get you away from whatever combination generated the nonsense price. For this route I’d also price Amtrak’s Empire Service between Buffalo and New York. Those Florida connections already consume most of the day before you add getting to the airport, security and the trip into Manhattan. BitMine Reports 6.06M ETH Holdings, With More Than 5.22M Staked

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: BitMine Immersion Technologies reported holdings of 6,060,506 ETH as of November 1 in a November 2 filing exhibit. Cryptocurrency balance sheets are sensitive to valuation dates.

The filing’s detailed token count is valuable precisely because it can be compared with later disclosures. It also exposes a blind spot in quick “ETH treasury” comparisons: two companies with identical token balances could have very different liquidity, financing and operational risk. Staking arrangements are an essential part of that distinction.

BitMine Immersion Technologies reported holdings of 6,060,506 ETH as of November 1 in a November 2 filing exhibit. The company said 5,227,037 ETH were staked, alongside smaller Bitcoin holdings, cash and other positions. These are issuer-disclosed balances at a specified date, not independently observed live holdings or a claim about Ethereum’s total currently liquid supply. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Staking means committing ETH to help secure Ethereum’s proof-of-stake network through validators or staking arrangements. Rewards depend on protocol conditions and operating choices, while access to staked assets can involve withdrawal queues or third-party arrangements. A substantial staked position may produce ongoing reward income, but it is not equivalent to holding the same amount in immediately transferable cash.

BitMine’s size makes its liquidity choices important. A large corporate ETH owner may hold a long-term position, but the market will still examine potential issuance, borrowing, staking income and the ability to rebalance. The issuer says its holdings represent a notable share of ETH’s supply; the comparison is a company calculation and should be tied to the supply and price assumptions used.

Ethereum has multiple sources of demand and risk: trading, staking, stablecoin settlement, application fees and long-term treasury allocation. A change in one part of that system should be tested against the others instead of promoted into a universal explanation. The market often overreacts when one publicly available metric is mistaken for the whole network economy.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on BitMine’s earlier ETH accumulation and staking disclosure and our Ethereum market reporting. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

Cryptocurrency balance sheets are sensitive to valuation dates. A dollar figure derived from the company’s ETH price assumption can change materially without any coins moving. Staking rewards should also be separated from share-price performance and company operating costs. A large token position does not by itself tell shareholders whether their ownership is increasing or being diluted.

For investors, the right comparison is between liquidity, expected holding period and obligations. Staked ETH, ETF shares and treasury holdings may all track the same underlying asset, but their exit routes and operating constraints differ. A useful report therefore asks not simply how much ETH is involved, but how readily the exposure can be changed when conditions deteriorate.

Look for future reports distinguishing unstaked liquidity, locked or queued balances, reward income, cash uses and equity issuance. Those measures will tell investors more about treasury resilience than the headline ETH number alone. For Ethereum, the interesting network question is whether institutional-sized staking concentrations affect how observers assess operator diversity and risk.

Elizabeth Holmes’ Partner Did an AMA About That Shower Scene, His Sniffing, and ‘You Can See Everything’

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Before this post even begins in earnest, let’s just get this out of the way: If you have not seen You Can See Everything, Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim’s documentary about convicted Theranos fraudster Elizabeth Holmes, you should absolutely stop reading right now, close this tab, and get yourself to a movie theater. In other words, spoilers ahead.

Alright, with that said: Billy Evans — Holmes’ romantic and business partner and the father of her two children — is basically doing his first interview since You Can See Everything was released last month, in the form of an “Ask Me Anything” on X. He abruptly opened the floor up for questions Monday evening, with a simple post, “AMA,” to which he quickly attached with the funniest possible follow-up: “Needs to be legally permissible.”

At first glance, Evans does not appear to hold back, offering responses to plenty of probing questions about You Can See Everything, as well as more mundane and random topics. But before getting to that, it’s worth a caveat: Evans is arguably the most compelling, strange, and unnerving presence in You Can See Everything, a film filled with compelling, strange, and unnerving presences. The doc poses, and struggles to answer, core questions like whether Evans is Holmes’ controlling Svengali (Holmes gave him power of attorney before she went to prison) or if Evans is actually an attention-seeking striver being used by Holmes (he comes from a wealthy family). Or, perhaps, it’s some ineffable third thing where Evans and Holmes are simply made for each other.

To that end, in the AMA, Evans said the biggest misconception from the film was the notion that he controls Holmes. “Anyone who actually knows her would find that idea hilarious,” he wrote. “She’s fiercely independent, strong-willed, and my best friend. We are partners in every sense. We challenge each other, disagree, and face the world together. Her closest friends are also some of my closest friends. If I ever mistreated her, they’d have my head.” Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

Pretty emphatic! But with Evans maybe a different question-and-answer offers a better lens through which to view this AMA: “Have you ever told a lie?” one person asked. It’s pointedly a question Fielder asked Holmes in the doc, and one she struggled to answer before replying “no” to a stunned Fielder. Evans, however, is succinct, responding: “Yes.”

With all that in mind, here are some of the highlights from Evans’ AMA.

To start, he has no regrets about participating in You Can See Everything, and when asked about his suspicious sniffing during the second half of the film (which some suspected was a result of cocaine use), he chalked it up to catching colds from his two kids and allergies. As for an already infamous early shot that showed Evans fully naked in the shower, he rebuffed the notion that such a scene could only happen with his permission. “False narrative,” he replied, before sharing a link to a recent People article in which “a source” said Evans felt “violated” by the scene.

Evans also tried to cast doubt on a crucial scene related to the question of whether Holmes knew Evans struck a deal with Fielder, Oppenheim, and A24 to receive a cut of You Can See Everything’s profits. At one point in the film, Holmes tells Fielder she’s not getting paid to participate in the film; but according to Fielder and Oppenheim, Evans struck a deal with them and A24 to receive some compensation. This allegedly happened during a meeting at the house of a friend of Evans and Holmes, after Holmes pointedly left the room to check on her kids.

Asked about the sequence, Evans bluntly replied, “Lmao, the house this was at was a single story. All you need to know.” (Evans and Holmes ultimately gave up their cut of the film’s profits before it was released.) Trending Stories Dane Cook’s Brother Robbed Him Blind. Another big issue Evans addressed was his having power of attorney over Holmes, and using her X account to tweet on her behalf. Evans said Holmes “knew exactly what we were doing,” adding, “We talk about everything, including X. Nothing happened behind her back. The POA was simply the legal mechanism that allowed me to act on her behalf while she’s incarcerated. It was not a substitute for her consent.”

Amidst all this, though, Evans’ AMA also contained a lot of fun and dumb stuff. Asked if any part of the film was scripted, Evans quipped, “Hardest part of filming was remembering my lines.” He shared his preference for dogs, but expressed his admiration for the “independence” of cats. His four favorite movies are The Fall, Alien, The Great Dictator, and Primer. And as for his favorite pop diva? “Chappell Roan has one of the best live sets I have ever seen. The crowd was amazing. I bought our engagement ring at a Taylor Swift reputation tour merch stand. Aretha Franklin has the best voice.”

On Location: How Coastal Newfoundland Set the Scene for Netflix's No. 1 Series ‘Below’

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A mysterious sea creature is captivating audiences on Netflix as the Josh Hartnett-led series Below remains the No. 1 show in Netflix’s global rankings. The six-episode thriller paints an eerie image of small-town life in Newfoundland, and viewers are intrigued by the mystique of the striking Canadian backdrop.

Despite the fictional giant squid terrorizing Snook’s Arm, the show pulls on elements of reality, from climate change affecting the Atlantic Canadian sea life to the government-run resettling of remote fishing towns, which started in 1954 and continues to this day. It’s that history that drew production designer Emmanuel Fréchette into the project.

“When I found out that more than 300 towns in Newfoundland had been resettled, I was amazed,” he tells Traveler. “The script is very interesting, mixing that reality with the mundane life in Newfoundland—and this creature!” After all, creator Jesse McKeown (The Umbrella Academy) had frequented the region for years, inspired to bring an authentic depiction of its culture to the screen.

That started behind the scenes as the cast and crew engaged in Newfoundland and Labrador’s most famous tradition. Visitors, who they called CFAs (or Come from Aways—this is also where Broadway hit musical draws its name), get “screeched in” by kissing a cod to become honorary citizens. So they all went to St. John’s Christian’s Pub to pucker up. “The guy there is such a great character, and he has a routine and speech that’s very entertaining," Fréchette says.

When it came to filming locations, the remote regions posed practical challenges, so the majority of the sets were built by production, a joy for Fréchette, who was trained as an architect. “I was stunned by the humble architecture, built by boat owners and builders,” he says of the homes’ low ceilings. “The structures are very endearing and make you think of a boat, so I wanted to follow that thread.”

That’s also what helped root the fantastical storyline into reality. “This was vernacular architecture—architecture without architects—just following the needs of the people out of survival, and I found that so beautiful,” he says. “So I really tried to mimic what I was seeing all around me that survived the decades.”

And it’s clearly had an effect on audiences. According to data provided by Booking.com, not only have searches for the province’s capital of St. John’s risen 16.9% by Americans on the platform compared to last year, but the coastal town of Twillingate, home to the floating icebergs, has skyrocketed 123.7%.

Here, Fréchette shares the filming locations from the popular Netflix series Below.

The show is set in the actual resettled town of Snook’s Arm, which had a population of 10 when it was approved for resettlement in 2017, according to the CBC. But its remote location would have been difficult to film in with such a big crew, so Brigus South—a town with a population of about 700 at last count in 2021—stood in.

“We picked that place because it had a connection with the water,” Fréchette says of the inlet going deep into the land. “Lots of the characters are dragged into the water, so it was great to have this presence for the eeriness.”

But to get the cozy town ready for its close-up took work, as the crew designed and built about 80% of what appears in the series, including the fish plant, Fishermen’s Hall, and wharf. The Penneys home was an existing home they covered, and the church was also a house that they retrofitted, adding the bell tower.

When Fréchette was scouting locations, he stayed in a hostel by the sea in Twillingate, about 275 miles northwest of the capital. “We’d be having a coffee with the window open, and suddenly we were freezing,” he recalls of a sudden drop in temperature from 68 degrees Fahrenheit to 50 degrees Fahrenheit. That’s when they’d look up and see an iceberg floating their way. “It lowers the temperature all around the bay—it’s such an amazing phenomenon.”

As it turns out, the Atlantic town sits along Iceberg Alley, where from late April through July, the remnants of broken glaciers glide down the coast. Many of the shots of Calvin (Hartnett) harvesting icebergs were actual footage from these massive blocks of ice naturally floating along.

The capital served as the home base for the cast and crew, who were spread out across hotels and home rentals during filming. But its Ocean Sciences Centre, about 30 minutes north of downtown, found the spotlight as one of the most striking structures in the series, the marine research center where Dr. Fonda Howander (Mackenzie Davis) worked.

While Fréchette admits it took some work to convince the operating facility to shoot there, he’s excited it worked out because “it really adds production value and is a great location.” After all, the circular wooden structure reflects that government building architect of the 1960s and early 1970s, “at a time we were dreaming and imagining things beyond what was possible to build today.” Reminiscent of designs by Canadian architects like Arthur Erikson, he adds that “it was ambitious, but it had a vision—and is fantastic!”

About 20 miles north of St. John’s at the top of the Avalon Peninsula sits Pouch Cove, a town of about 2,000 residents. The art director told Fréchette about the Pouch Cove Heritage Society, a historical building that was closed since they were fundraising to preserve it, so one weekend, they went up to visit. “The floor was sunken in and it was structurally unsafe,” he says.

The show’s head carpenter and engineer got to work, fixing it up, temporarily transforming it into the interior of Below’s Fisherman’s Hall, while permanently restoring it for the community. “It was a win-win because they were happy to get the money from the location, but also the work we did on it was helpful for them.”

When Calvin returns to his abandoned childhood town of Little Pot Rock, that dilapidated setting was an actual resettled town, about a 30-minute drive down from Pouch Cove on a dirt road that the production had to grade in order to shoot there.

“It was quite tricky, but just stunning—really wild and rugged,” Fréchette says, calling it “the most amazing location.” All that was there was one tiny house, about 12 by 20 feet, but it never showed up on camera. Instead, the production custom built all of the Budgell family’s dwellings. But the area can be visited as part of a 4.3-mile hike along the East Coast Trail.

The fishing village of Bay Bulls is home to North America’s largest Atlantic puffin colony, as well as a prime whale watching destination. But for Below, it was all about the functional purpose of its Pennecon Marine Base. Though it never shows up on screen, its industrial harbor was essential in order to dock the boat used as the research vessel since it had a draft (depth) of nearly 10 feet, and they needed an appropriate spot that the cast and crew could board. Also shot here were the exterior scenes of the submersible, which was hung from the boat by a crane and lifted onto the dock.

Pittman’s fish farm played an integral part of the plot, as Calvin confirmed that the sea creature was real—but it turns out the location was landlocked. “It was very complicated to do this on the sea because we’d have to deal with tides and storms, so we decided to cheat it on a lake in Brigus,” he says of the town that’s actually about 60 miles northwest from Brigus South. There was no actual fish farm there, so they custom-built the floating dock and cabin.

While the outside of Linda’s Bar was filmed using a retrofitted Brigus South home, the interior was actually the Rising Tide Convenience shop in the town of Harbour Main, about 33 miles southwest of St. John’s. The local business, best known for its homemade ice cream, “looked great inside, so we dressed it up,” Fréchette says, taking extra care to ensure the exterior entrance they built flowed seamlessly between cuts. As for Noah (Kaleb Horn)’s on-screen screech-in? That was a prop, but an actual frozen fish to ensure the fish kiss was genuine.

As the most discerning, up-to-the-minute voice in all things travel, Condé Nast Traveler is the global citizen’s bible and muse, offering both inspiration and vital intel. We understand that time is the greatest luxury, which is why Condé Nast Traveler mines its network of experts and influencers so that you never waste a meal, a drink, or a hotel stay wherever you are in the world.

Birmingham City 3-0 Millwall: Highlights, man of the match, stats as Max Bird solves August Priske problem

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

By Carter White | 03 Nov 2026 21:53, Last updated: 03 Nov 2026 22:40

Birmingham City recorded their third straight home Championship success with a 3-0 victory over Millwall at St Andrew's @ Knighthead Park on Tuesday night.

After netting seven goals in their previous two home matches, Blues were at their attacking best once again from the off in the Second City, as Alex Cochrane fired his side ahead within the opening minute.

Making his full second-tier debut for Birmingham, Max Bird doubled the hosts' advantage at the 16-minute mark before providing the assist for Jay Stansfield's second of the season to put Millwall to rest in the contest.

The Lions were improved in the second period following a half-time formation change, however, the damage had already been inflicted by a Blues side who are allowing their supporters to dream of a long-awaited Premier League return.

Carter White live from St Andrew's @ Knighthead Park

Despite high-profile absentees in goal and up front in the form of James Beadle and August Priske, Birmingham enjoyed another comfortable home victory after smashing Preston North End and Queens Park Rangers in October.

As was the case against bottom side Preston, Blues raced into a three-goal lead before the half-time break, all-but deciding the destination of the three points before the second period had even started.

It is now five wins in the past six for Chris Davies's troops, who are continuing to prove their worth as serious promotion candidates, moving into the top two courtesy of an impressive Second City success.

As for the visitors, Millwall have now lost four of their opening seven away contests of the Championship campaign, equalling the number of defeats that they endured across 23 outings during the 2025-26 term.

Alex Neil's men have painfully misplaced their winning formula on the road and it has led to another chastening loss at the hands of Blues, who smashed four past the Lions in last season's contest at St Andrew's.

Both Taylor and Jake Cooper are quick to recognise the threat of the £20m man, however, Stansfield gets the better of them both, earning a yard to the left and placing a strike past Crocombe at his near post.

Priske's absence this evening resulted in Stansfield operating as the central striker, meaning that Davies had a hole to fill at the tip of midfield, and it is safe to say that the Birmingham boss made the right selection.

The 26-year-old netted his first competitive goal since February to double Birmingham's lead during the opening 20 minutes before turning provider for his side's third, feeding an excellent ball through to goalscorer Stansfield.

Blues have struggled when leading goalscorer Priske has missed out at points during the early stages of this term but that was not the case tonight, with Bird proving that Davies's side can survive without their Danish marksman.

Millwall are back in the West Midlands on the same day, with the wounded Lions facing West Bromwich Albion at The Hawthorns, where the visiting faithful will demand a much-improved performance.

Air Tahiti Nui Will Offer Free Starlink Wi-Fi On Its Boeing 787s As Of 2027

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Air Tahiti Nui has just announced a massive upgrade to its inflight connectivity, as the airline will be rolling out free Starlink Wi-Fi across its (small) fleet in 2027.

Air Tahiti Nui has revealed that it plans to install Starlink Wi-Fi on its entire fleet, which consists of four Boeing 787-9s. The airline plans to offer this service for free to passengers in all travel classes.

Starlink will be introduced in 2027, though no timeline beyond that is known yet, so it could be early or late in the year. Presumably once installation starts, it’ll be completed very quickly — four planes could easily be fitted with Starlink in a single week.

Air Tahiti Nui operates some very long flights — including from Papeete (PPT) to Paris (CDG) via Los Angeles (LAX) so having fast and free connectivity is super valuable. While so many airlines market their Wi-Fi as being high speed, Starlink is the only product currently in service where you can consistently stream videos, etc.

Currently, Air Tahiti Nui’s Dreamliners are equipped with Panasonic Wi-Fi, which actually has relatively decent speeds. The catch is that Air Tahiti Nui chooses to have insanely high pricing based primarily on data usage, rather than just offering a full flight pass for a fixed amount.

Below is Air Tahiti Nui’s current pricing chart for Wi-Fi… could they make this any more complicated? $28 for 100 MB of data is absurd, and $98 for unlimited data is even more ridiculous.

This kind of pricing just strikes me as being so stupid. All you end up doing is frustrating passengers, and I also have to imagine this isn’t actually the system that maximizes usage. It’s weird to have a provider with speeds that are actually semi-usable (Panasonic is better than OnAir), and to then do this with it.

So anyway, Starlink will simply be a massive upgrade for Air Tahiti Nui in every way. Speaking of which, I’d sure love to return to French Polynesia at some point (and fly that all-business class Air Tahiti turboprop, while I’m at it).

In 2027, Air Tahiti Nui intends to install Starlink Wi-Fi on its fleet of four Dreamliners. It remains to be seen exactly when in 2027 it’ll happen, but this represents a huge upgrade to the passenger experience. The airline currently offers Panasonic Wi-Fi with awful data-based pricing (or an unlimited package that will run you close to $100).

What do you make of Air Tahiti Nui introducing Starlink?

Bridget Fonda Makes Rare Appearance 24 Years After Last Acting Role

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Single White Female actress Bridget Fonda made a rare appearance in Los Angeles Nov. 2, over 24 years after she retired from acting following the 2002 mini series Snow Queen.

Bridget Fonda is finding a life away from Hollywood.

The Single White Female actress made a rare public appearance in Los Angeles Nov. 2, over 24 years after she last graced the screen in the 2002 mini series Snow Queen.

The 62-year-old kept it casual for her lunch outing with a friend, wearing a white T-shirt paired with black sweats and an oversized yellow button-down shirt. She completed her look with a black bag, white sneakers and her hair in a ponytail.

Fonda has done her best to stay out of the spotlight since retiring from acting, instead keeping her focus on her 23-year marriage to musician and composer Danny Elfman and raising their 21-year-old son Oliver Elfman.

And as the Jackie Brown actress admitted, she has no desire to return to her acting career, telling a photographer in 2023, per the Daily Mail, “It’s too nice being a civilian.”

Being a civilian has also helped set her apart from the rest of her famous family, as Fonda is the granddaughter of classic Hollywood star Henry Fonda, the daughter of actor Peter Fonda and his first wife Susan Brewer, as well as the niece of actress Jane Fonda.

And despite growing up among Hollywood royalty and raised by a father who was a leading figure in the counterculture movement with his 1969 film Easy Rider, Fonda has been candid about how normal her childhood was.

“People imagine that I was dumped in the corner of a series of drug-fuelled orgies, and that we were like savages in the bush,” she told The Guardian in 2000, “but it wasn't like that at all. I grew up around adults who were imaginative and spontaneous, sure, and it was fantastic to go on that ride.”

Though she had an unconventional childhood—recalling how she would hang out on Malibu beach with her godfather Larry Hagman—Fonda’s parents also emphasized “discipline” with her.

“I called everyone mister and missus, like friends' parents and teachers, even though it was a time when everyone was insisting on being called by their first names,” Fonda said. “I always washed my hands before I ate, and used a napkin.”

“My dad may have been a hippy biker in the public mind,” she added, “but he was always a classy, well-turned-out hippy.”

For a deep dive into more stars who have stepped away from Hollywood, keep reading.

After the Mask of Zorro actor suffered a near-fatal heart attack in 2019, he moved back to his native Málago, Spain, to focus on his love of theater.

“It changed the way I look at life,” Banderas told The Times in March 2026. “Faced with death, it made me look back and realise that I am, in fact, a theater actor.”

Now, the Tony nominee—who still occasionally takes on TV and film projects—owns a theater venue and gets involved with the local community.

“I have never been so happy,” he continued. “It keeps me in touch with my roots, my neighbourhood, my people. I haven’t lost that connection over the years. Quite the opposite.”

Before landing a role in Prime Video's Every Year After, The Girl Next Door actress took a four-year break from acting, leaving L.A. to raise her two kids with husband Dion Phaneuf.

"I realized I worked all four years through our first child and it was really hard," Cuthbert confessed in June 2026 during an appearance on Today, "to separate that mom from the working person I was. So, when we had our second, I just felt like I didn't want to waste any second of it and I didn't want to be on set."

Cuthbert—best known for starring in '00s films like Old School as well as hit TV shows including 24 and Happy Endings—added, "I just felt like I needed to be at home with the kids and I enjoyed every minute."

The actress, known for her roles on hit TV dramas like Fire Country, Numb3rs and Rescue Me, shared in July 2026 that she sold her Los Angeles home after living there for 30 years to embark on her "third chapter" somewhere far away from Hollywood.

"I came here chasing a dream that I am still shocked worked out," she wrote on Instagram. "And I also got to raise babies in the sun and the mountains and enjoy this beautiful home and attend public schools ?? Pinch me I’m overwhelmed by the blessings and hope, really hope I gave enough back. LA will always be where my family is from."

The Ellen DeGeneres Show host's eponymous talk show ended in 2022 after a 19-season run amid allegations of a toxic work environment that prompted an internal investigation, multiple firings of executives and public ire.

DeGeneres returned to the spotlight for her final standup special For Your Approval, which released on Netflix in September 2024. During the lead-up to that special, she revealed she would be leaving Hollywood altogether.

"This is the last time you’re going to see me," she told a crowd during her Ellen’s Last Stand…Up tour, per SFGate. “After my Netflix special, I’m done."

DeGeneres and her wife Portia de Rossi have since moved to the Cotswolds, deciding to stay put in November 2024 after Donald Trump's reelection, the talk show host told to Richard Bacon in a July 2025 conversation at the Everyman theater in Cheltenham.

"It's absolutely beautiful," she gushed about the English countryside, per the BBC. "We're just not used to seeing this kind of beauty. The villages and the towns and the architecture—everything you see is charming and it's just a simpler way of life.

"It's clean," DeGeneres continued. "Everything here is just better—the way animals are treated, people are polite. I just love it here. We moved here in November, which was not the ideal time, but I saw snow for the first time in my life. We love it here. Portia flew her horses here, and I have chickens, and we had sheep for about two weeks."

One of the reasons the America's Next Top Model host moved to Australia? To stay on top of her ice cream brand."So I have this ice cream company, SMiZE and Dream," Banks explained on an April 2025 episode of Today with Jenna & Friends. “But we've been more what we call B2B, like on an airplane and on cruises. I’ve been going to Australia a lot because we were making a lot of our ice cream there in a big facility doing our recipes. And I just fell in love with it every time I went back and went back."Not only was she happy there, but she also knew it was a good market for her business."The three countries that eat the most ice cream?" she continued. "America, New Zealand and Australia."So, the supermodel moved to Australia with her son York (his dad is her ex Erik Asla) and boyfriend Louis Bélanger-Martin, noting, "Australia has been a dream come true for me and my family."

While it's been a minute since the Desperate Housewives star lived on Wisteria Lane, she's since found a new address IRL."I had my whole adult life here," Eva told Marie Claire in November 2024 about leaving Los Angeles. "But even before [the pandemic], it was changing. The vibe was different. And then Covid happened, and it pushed it over the edge. Whether it’s the homelessness or the taxes, not that I want to s--t on California—it just feels like this chapter in my life is done now."So for the past few years, the actress, her husband José Bastón and their son Santiago have split their time between Mexico and Spain."I’m privileged," she continued. "I get to escape and go somewhere. Most Americans aren’t so lucky. They’re going to be stuck in this dystopian country, and my anxiety and sadness is for them."However, Eva noted her move wasn't because of President Trump's re-election."I didn't leave because of the political environment," the Flamin' Hot director said on The View: Behind the Table podcast in November 2024. "I left because my work took me there since Land of Women—shooting six months in Catalonia, then four months in Mexico for Searching for Mexico, then back to Spain. Now I've been there for years. So I just don't like that it's politicized."Since moving, Eva has continued to stay busy, with recent projects including her roles in Only Murders in the Building and Alexander and the Terrible, Horrible, No Good, Very Bad Road Trip.

The actor has gone from Chicago to Spain."My wife is Spanish, and she gave me about seven years here," Richard said of Galicia native Alejandra Silva on a November 2024 episode of The Tonight Show Starring Jimmy Fallon. "And so we're going to spend some years in Madrid."He also expressed his excitement over what the move would give the couple's young sons Alexander and James."Our kids are bilingual," Richard, who also has an adult son Homer with ex Carey Lowell, added, "so they're going to flourish there."Indeed, it seems the whole family has."The truth is that you are seeing us in our momentum," The Agency: Central Intelligence star told Elle España in January 2025. "We are happier than ever."

From AM to PM, the singer is living in Paris "My husband is French, and we've been together for over seven or eight years," Milian, who is married to singer Matt Pokora, said on a November 2024 episode of The Jennifer Hudson Show. "And for the last three or four years we were commuting back and forth every six months."However, they decided to officially say au revoir to the U.S. so her kids—who include daughter Violet from her former marriage with The-Dream and sons Isaiah and Kenna from her union with Pokora—could study in France."They're all going to school in Paris," she continued. "I was like, 'What better than an opportunity than that?"And life for the family appears to be magnifique."My kids are happy there," the Dexter: Original Sin actress told E! in December 2024. "They know French. We go to the park. I have an active lifestyle that's really nice during the day. Then at night time, I do my work because I work hours ahead. It's a nice, healthy balance for me."

The Mean Girls star is tired of rumors starting about why she left Los Angeles.So, she set the record straight while looking back at her hectic work schedule and partying in the aughts. "I feel like some of [my work] got overshadowed by paparazzi and all that kind of stuff when I was younger, and that’s kind of annoying," the Parent Trap alum told Bustle in March 2024. "I wish that part didn’t happen. I feel like that kind of took on a life of its own. So that’s why I wanted to disappear. I was like, 'Unless there’s no story here, they’re not going to focus on just my work.'"Lohan moved abroad—first to London then to Dubai (also opening beach clubs in Greece). However, there was another reason she sought a life away from the spotlight.

"I was like, 'What if I never fall in love? What if this never happens?'" she added. "And it took me just taking time for me for all those doors to open and the ‘yeses’ to come—the things I wanted to say 'yes' to."Of course, Lohan did find love with Bader Shammas, the pair marrying in 2022 and welcoming son Luai the following year. They still live in Dubai, but the Freaky Friday actress hasn't left the Hollywood industry completely, starring in movies like Our Little Secret, Irish Wish and Freakier Friday.

For Hartnett, the decision to leave Hollywood came after some self-reflection."Hollywood is more than a place; it's like a state of mind," the Black Mirror actor told People in July 2024. "If you're around people who are constantly talking about the movie business, your life is going to be pretty much one note. And I've never wanted that."Instead, he wanted more."I've always wanted to be around ... people generally doing lots of other things that would bring my mind off of my work and force me to grow in different directions," Hartnett added. "To me, that's the spice of life."So, the Black Hawk Down alum, his wife Tamsin Egerton and their four kids moved to England's Hampshire countryside."Time passes quickly," he told The Guardian in July 2024. "With four children, you have so much to do. In a way, less is happening. But more of the important stuff is happening."Still, Hartnett has stayed plenty busy and landed roles in Verity, Fight or Flight and Trap.

If you haven't checked your social network lately, then you might not know that Eisenberg doesn't live in L.A."I’ve lived in Indiana for the last decade on-and-off," the A Real Pain star told CBS News Sunday Morning in February 2025. "That’s where I feel the most comfortable. I’m not somebody who wants to surround myself in an industry that just feels kind of unstable to me."And while he's an Oscar nominee, he lives a pretty normal life with his wife Anna Strout and son Banner."I'm not on social media, and I'm not a person who's on the phone with industry people all the time," Eisenberg also shared. "So, my life feels pretty separate. My wife is a teacher. My kid doesn't really even watch movies. So, I'm not like knee-deep in the world of constantly trying to assess my own status in an industry."

After appearing in such projects as Buffy the Vampire Slayer and 2000's Bring It On, Dushku revealed in September 2024 that she had become certified in psychedelic-assisted therapy and is furthering her studies as a mental health professional.

"I had the means to shift directions and choose a course in my life that focused on healing myself so that I could help heal others," she told Boston Magazine. "I would be remiss if I didn’t now share the transformation and the peace and the passion that I have. This is just absolutely so clearly my real calling, my real purpose."

The Flintstones star revealed that she moved to Ireland in January 2025 with her youngest child Clay and was in the process of getting her Irish citizenship as her grandparents are from the European country. But while Rosie cited recent political developments as her reason for leaving, she didn’t rule out an eventual return home.

"I miss my other kids," she said in a video on TikTok. "I miss my friends. I miss many things about life there at home and I'm trying to find a home here in in this beautiful country. And when it is safe for all citizens to have equal rights there in America, that's when we will consider coming back."

The Ant-Man and the Wasp star announced she's taking an indefinite hiatus from acting after 20 years in the industry.

"I am so filled with joy and contentment today as I live out my vision," Lilly wrote on Instagram in June 2024. "I feel so grateful for my blessings. Stepping away from what seems like the obvious choice (wealth and fame) can feel scary at times, but stepping into your dharma replaces the fear with fulfillment."

But she isn't necessarily shutting the door, either, adding, "I might return to Hollywood one day, but, for now, this is where I belong."

The Vampire Diaries alum hasn't sunk his teeth into a new role since Netflix's 2019 series V-Wars. Instead, he has dug a little deeper into a passion project, namely his mission to combat climate change by improving the world's soil.

"I stepped away from acting a little over four years ago to raise my kids, build my companies and get these films launched," Somerhalder, who shares two children with wife Nikki Reed, told E! News in November 2023 of launching the 2020 documentary Kiss the Ground and its follow up Common Ground, each detailing the need for regenerative farming.

When he looks years down the road, the actor continued, "I will be a rancher and building legacy brands, whether it's my bourbon or my health and wellness company, and the regenerative agriculture and healthy soil management practices that I live by and our family lives by. That's where my life was going. So when people say 'Why do you care?' That's why I care. Because that's who I am, that's what I'm going to become."

On a February 2021 episode of her podcast Empty Inside, the iCarly alum told guest Anna Faris that she decided to stop acting a few years back and is now focused on opportunities in writing and directing, in addition to hosting the podcast.

"My experience with acting is, I'm so ashamed of the parts I've done in the past," revealed McCurdy, who detailed her experience in her 2022 memoir I'm Glad My Mom Died. "I resent my career in a lot of ways. I feel so unfulfilled by the roles that I played and felt like it was the most cheesy, embarrassing. I did the shows that I was on from like 13 to 21, and by 15, I was already embarrassed. My friends at 15, they're not like, 'Oh, cool, you're on this Nickelodeon show.' It was embarrassing."

Though she hasn't totally written off a second act. "I do feel like only through writing the book have I gotten to a place where I think there might be a way of exploring acting that doesn't carry that baggage that I carried with me for so long," she told E! News in October 2022. "Maybe if I write something for myself. I think that'd really be maybe one of the only ways I could kind of try exploring it again."

This felt like something of a no-brainer. If we had a shot to marry Prince Harry, dedicate our life to the philanthropic causes that matter most to us and gain access to the Queen's impressive collection of tiaras, bidding ta-ta to Tinseltown would feel like an okay sacrifice.

Following the path Grace Kelly took from Hollywood to the Palace of Monaco, Meghan left behind her home in Toronto—and her breakout role on Suits—for a life in The Firm. Though, now that she and Harry have shed their senior roles and live in Montecito, Calif., she's edging back into the business thanks to business moves like their multi-year production deal with Netflix. The Duchess of Sussex released her 2022 docuseries Harry & Meghan as well as her 2025 lifestyle show With Love, Meghan on the streaming service. She's also launched podcasts and her brand As Ever.

Having made upwards of 40 movies since her debut in 1994's The Mask, the actress was long overdue for a break when filming wrapped on 2014's Annie.

"I just decided that I wanted different things out of my life," she explained to pal Gwyneth Paltrow of her ultimate act of self-care. "I had gone so hard for so long, working, making films and it's such a grind. When you're making a movie—it's a perfect excuse—they own you. You're there for 12 hours a day for months on end and you have no time for anything else."

And there was plenty more she'd like to explore, from writing (she followed up her 2013 New York Times best-seller The Body Book with 2016's The Longevity Book) to starting a family with husband Benji Madden. But while Cameron confessed to InStyle in 2019, "I don't miss performing. Right now I'm looking at the landscape of wellness and all that," apparently she could be swayed.

"We just begged and pleased on my knees, like, 'Just give the people one more again,'" Jamie Foxx told E! News of getting Diaz to sign on for the 2025 Netflix film Back in Action. "We love her, we've been waiting on her and this is just gonna be fantastic."

The actor insisted there'd be no encore after he finished his five-year run on Empire. When Extra asked Howard about his future ahead of the musical drama series' sixth and final season in 2019, he responded, "Oh, I'm done with acting. I'm done pretending."

And though he returned to set for a handful of projects, the Oscar nominee again announced his plans to quit acting while promoting his Peacock series The Best Man: The Final Chapters in 2022. "I've gotten to the point where now I've given the very best that I have as an actor," he told ET. "Now I'm enjoying watching other new talent come around, and I don't want to do an impersonation of myself."

Despite these comments, though, Howard has since appeared in a few more shows and movies, including the TV series Fight Night: The Million Dollar Heist and Netflix film Shirley, with multiple projects lined up in the future.

Once his time as King Joffrey came to a sudden, purple-faced end in 2014, the then-21-year-old told EW he was getting out of the game. "I've been acting since age 8," the Game of Thrones star explained of his decision. "I just stopped enjoying it as much as I used to."

No longer a child star, a bit of the magic had worn off. "Now there's the prospect of doing it for a living, whereas up until now it was always something I did for recreation with my friends, or in the summer for some fun," Gleeson continued. "I enjoyed it. When you make a living from something, it changes your relationship with it. It's not like I hate it, it's just not what I want to do."

But after a six-year break, he was ready to resume his reign, joining the cast of BBC's series Out of Her Mind in 2020. Since then, Jack has also appeared in movies like Rebecca's Boyfriend and In the Land of Saints and Sinners as well as TV shows like Safe Harbor and The Famous Five.

When Fast Times at Ridgemont High's dream girl (Jennifer Aniston took on her part in September 2020's virtual table read) wed fellow actor Kevin Kline in 1989, they "agreed to alternate so that we're never working at the same time," he told Playboy of their plan to care for son Owen and daughter Greta (a singer who now goes by the stage name Frankie Cosmos).

However, he continued, "whenever it's been her slot to work, Cates has chosen to stay with the children." Though she made a cameo in pal Jennifer Jason Leigh's 2001 indie The Anniversary Party, Phoebe devotes most of her time to operating her New York City boutique Blue Tree.

At the height of his Ghostbusters and Honey, I... fame, the '80s star stepped away from filmmaking not long after his wife passed away from breast cancer in 1991 to focus on raising his kids Rachel and Mitchell.

Though Moranis hasn't had a live-action movie role since 1997, even passing on a cameo in Paul Feig's 2016 Ghostbusters remake, he's remained a treasured cultural icon, as evidenced by the outrage over reports that he'd been assaulted while walking in New York City in 2020.Thankfully there's good news for fans: He'll make his triumphant return alongside Josh Gad in the forthcoming Disney reboot Shrunk.

Once she wed fashion designer Adam Kimmel in 2010, the Never Been Kissed standout hinted that she was maybe kinda done with movies. "Ninety percent of acting roles involve so much sexual stuff with other people, and I don't want to do that," she explained to Vogue. "It's such a strange fire to play with, and our relationship is surely strong enough to handle it, but if you're going to walk through fire, there has to be something incredible on the other side."

Her decision was solidified after son Martin joined older sister Louisanna in 2014. "I don't do movie stuff anymore. I am totally an outsider! I … am just a mom and an outsider," she noted to Us Weekly at a 2016 event, explaining that she helps Kimmel with his business and paints on the side. "I am just focused on my kids. I think that's mainly why I stopped."

She'd done Ally McBeal. And Nip/Tuck. Then Arrested Development and finally Scandal when the Aussie realized that maybe she was ready to turn the dial.

"I was approaching 45 and I just kind of…was wondering is there something that I could tackle now that I've never done before that would be really challenging and different," she explained on wife Ellen DeGeneres' eponymous talk show in 2018. "I kind of knew what acting would look like for me for the next 10, 20 years, so I decided to quit and start a business."

Her consumer-art company, General Public, already a work in progress, she had just one piece to finish.

"I called Mitch Hurwitz, who's the creator of Arrested Development and I said, 'If there is a season 5, I won't be doing it because I quit acting. And he seemed really understanding and he totally got it. We had a great conversation, and then he wrote me into five episodes."

To be fair, once you've won three Academy Awards, what's left to accomplish? Shortly after receiving his sixth Oscar nod for his final film, 2017's Phantom Thread, the thespian had his rep issue a statement informing fans that he "will no longer be working as an actor. He is immensely grateful to all of his collaborators and audiences over the many years. This is a private decision and neither he nor his representatives will make any further comment on this subject."

But just when Day-Lewis thought he was out, his son pulled him back in. In October 2024, production company Focus Features announced the Oscar winner would star in 2025's Anemone, the film he wrote alongside son Ronan Day-Lewis.

The Good Luck Charlie alum took a step back from the spotlight in 2018 when she began pursuing a master's degree from MIT, later earning a PhD from the institution. She also enrolled in Harvard Law School.

And as if that weren't enough, Mendler launched her own startup Northwood Space, which aims to build ground satellite stations to help send and receive data from space, becoming CEO of the brand in February 2023.

JetBlue raises bag fees for third time in a year as fuel costs bite

📅 2026-11-03  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

JetBlue Airways has raised the price of checking a bag for many customers for the third time in less than a year as high fuel costs continue to eat into the industry's profits.

Checking a first bag for travelers booked in economy will now be $50, up from $45, if purchased more than 24 hours before departure during a nonpeak travel period. If it's paid within 24 hours of departure, the fee will be $60. During a peak period, such as during the holidays or the summer, it will be $55 if purchased more than 24 hours before departure, up from $49, and $70 for within 24 hours of departure.

There are exceptions to the checked bag fees, such as for customers who hold a co-branded JetBlue credit card or who hold elite frequent flyer status.

"We're adjusting checked bag fees to help offset rising fuel and operating costs," JetBlue said in a statement. "This helps us keep our base fares competitive while giving customers the choice to pay for a checked bag only if they need one."

The price of jet fuel, airlines' biggest cost after labor, has roughly doubled this year since the start of the Iran war in February, costing airlines billions and driving up the price of tickets. Jet fuel was going for $4.44 a gallon on Tuesday for the U.S. Gulf of Mexico, according to the U.S. Energy Information Administration.

Other airlines often follow when one carrier raises fees. Major U.S. airlines did so earlier this year after JetBlue raised its bag fees.

The changes were reported earlier by the Thrifty Traveler blog.

Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: Teaching children about high deductible health plans

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

It’s my thinking that with most things in life you will get punished financially for chronically making decisions that lead to more dependency on others or institutions. I think it’s over the general public’s head when one starts talking about “triple tax advantaged”, investing instead of using HSA money (but saving big ticket receipts just in case) and paying out of pocket for any services you can afford.
It's also not appropriate for the general public to do this. Saving receipts for later reimbursement is only the right strategy if you are maxing out your tax-advantaged accounts, which isn't common. And it is even less common for investors who are starting out with an HSA, who are likely to be early in their careers and thus to have lower salaries and debt payments which limit their savings.

The most important general lesson is that you should max out the HSA in preference to any other investment (except a 401(k) with an employer match); the HSA is a tax-free account with a match from the IRS.
My employer provides a choice of a very low deductible Cadillac medical plan and a HDHP with HSA plan - both nearly fully paid for by the company. I realize this is not the case at many companies but in my case - it makes no sense to pick the HDHP paying thousands in medical costs today simply to stash away a few dollars in an HSA - yet so many do it because they hear how greatly tax advantaged HSAs are. The OP opened with the general statement that they are a big fan of high deductible plans. I remain a big fan of low deductible plans and paying almost zero (other than a few copays) for medical costs all year.

Montefiore Einstein Comprehensive Cancer Center Awarded $10 Million Grant to Investigate Breast Cancer Relapse

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Collaborative research projects will focus on studies of cancer dormancy and metastasis

BRONX, N.Y., Nov. 3, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- The National Cancer Institute (NCI) has awarded a multidisciplinary and multi-institutional team led by Julio A. Aguirre-Ghiso, Ph.D., of Montefiore Einstein Comprehensive Cancer Center (MECCC), a five-year, $10 million grant to uncover the biological processes that drive breast cancer relapse and identify new opportunities to predict and prevent recurrence.

Up to an estimated 685,000 people die from breast cancer globally each year, according to the NCI, with nearly all fatal cases resulting from disease that has returned in other organs years or even decades after surgery, chemotherapy, or radiation. Research suggests that even a small, localized tumor may have already generated micrometastasesâsmall amounts of cancer cells that have spread beyond the original tumor but cannot be detected by screening or diagnostic tests. Because these cells may have traveled beyond their initial tumor location, they can remain in the body after the primary tumor is removed or treated, lie dormant for years, and later "reawaken" and cause breast cancer to recur.

Exploring Cancer Dormancy The new research will address one of the least understood and most consequential areas of cancer biology: the characteristics of early disseminated cancer cells that survive surgery and other treatments by remaining dormant. Understanding these cells' distinguishing features could help scientists detect them and develop therapies that eliminate them before the cancer returns.

"For two decades, we have been pioneering a new era of cancer research that could enable more precise, proactive treatments designed to prevent recurrence and improve long-term remission," said Dr. Aguirre-Ghiso, professor of cell biology, of oncology, and of medicine and the Rose C. Falkenstein Chair in Cancer Research at Albert Einstein College of Medicine. "By closing gaps in our understanding of breast cancer relapse, our goal is to identify who is most at risk of recurrence and find ways to intercept cancer before it returns." Dr. Aguirre-Ghiso also serves as MECCC's associate director of basic science, co-leader of the Tumor Microenvironment & Metastasis Program, and director of the Cancer Dormancy Institute.

The researchers will analyze clinical data and tissue samples from people with ductal carcinoma in situ (DCIS), an early, non-invasive form of breast cancer. They will use the findings to investigate the mechanisms that allow cancer cells to spread early and determine whether specific characteristics of those cells are associated with subsequent relapse.

Combining Expertise Across DisciplinesThe multidisciplinary project brings together investigators with expertise in cancer biology, pathology, epidemiology, imaging, and bioinformatics.

"Montefiore Einstein is bringing together researchers across institutions to tackle a question that no single discipline can answer: why do some breast cancer cells spread early and reappear years later?" said Dr. Aguirre-Ghiso. "By connecting patient data and tissue samples with laboratory studies and advanced imaging, we hope to identify who is at risk of relapse and find ways to prevent it."

The study, "Mechanisms of Early Breast Cancer Local and Distant Relapse," is supported by NCI grant 1P01CA295557.

About Montefiore Einstein Comprehensive Cancer CenterMontefiore Einstein Comprehensive Cancer Center (MECCC) is a National Cancer Institute (NCI)-designated comprehensive cancer center and a national leader in cancer research and clinical care located in the racially and ethnically diverse borough of the Bronx, N.Y. MECCC combines the exceptional science of Albert Einstein College of Medicine with the multidisciplinary and team-based approach to cancer clinical care at Montefiore Health System. Founded in 1971 and an NCI-designated cancer center since 1972, MECCC is redefining excellence in cancer research, clinical care, education and training, and community outreach and engagement. Its mission is to reduce the burden of cancer for all, especially people from historically underrepresented groups.

About Albert Einstein College of MedicineAlbert Einstein College of Medicine is one of the nation's premier academic centers for basic science research, clinical investigation, and biomedical education. Located in the Bronx, Einstein is home to nearly 1,000 M.D., Ph.D., and M.D./Ph.D. students and more than 2,000 full-time faculty members. Einstein receives approximately $200M in funding from the National Institutes of Health (NIH) each year and houses six NIH-funded research centers, in cancer, intellectual and developmental disabilities, clinical and translational research, AIDS, and two in diabetes. In partnership with Montefiore Health System, Einstein advances clinical and translational research to accelerate the pace at which new discoveries become the treatments that benefit patients. For more information, please visit einsteinmed.edu, and follow us on Instagram, LinkedIn, Twitter, Facebook, and view us on YouTube.

SOURCE Montefiore Einstein Comprehensive Cancer Center

Lynn Henning Sparks Firestorm With Controversial Jack Campbell Medical Request

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Detroit Lions fans are pushing back against longtime Detroit sports columnist Lynn Henning after he publicly called on Jack Campbell's family to provide more information about the injured linebacker's medical condition.

Campbell remains the subject of widespread concern following Sunday's frightening incident against the Minnesota Vikings. However, the Lions have made it clear that additional medical details will remain private out of respect for Campbell and his family.

That apparently wasn't enough for Henning, whose Tuesday morning post on X sparked a wave of angry responses from fans who believe the family deserves privacy during an incredibly difficult time.

In a Tuesday morning post on X, Henning issued what he described as a public appeal directly to Campbell's family.

"A proper appeal to the Jack Campbell family: Please inform the football world about what doctors have so far determined. People care, fundamentally, about Jack Campbell. They also wish to know anything that pertains to the family's and team's early indicators of optimism. This is not intrusive. It's being asked by those who saw the event and who care mightily about a man's health and future."

Henning's argument was that fans are genuinely concerned about Campbell and want to understand the medical basis for the team's encouraging updates.

But several people responding to the post strongly disagreed with his approach, particularly the suggestion that Campbell's family should publicly disclose additional medical information.

One fan, posting under the username @waltwhite67917, questioned whether Henning's request was appropriate.

"Respect the privacy of jack and his family, Lynn. Poking and prying, demanding confidential medical information isn't very professional."

Henning defended his position in a lengthy response, arguing that the public nature of Campbell's injury made additional information appropriate.

"Sure it's professional. Jack Campbell was hurt Sunday, visibly and traumatically, on the national sports stage."

Henning continued by arguing that two days without a detailed explanation did little to ease public anxiety, maintaining that seeking additional information demonstrated concern and compassion.

That explanation did not satisfy several other commenters.

"Lynn, this is an awkward and disappointing lack of empathy from you. I hope you'll reconsider," wrote @Metz4Real.

Another fan, @DetroitC13531, offered a simpler response.

"Its none of our business. If the Campbell's are like my family, they are afraid to say anything because they don't want to 'jynx' anything."

Others were considerably less diplomatic, with one responding, "Don't be this guy," while another posted a profanity-filled rebuke insisting that fans were owed nothing.

The criticism reflects a disagreement over where legitimate public concern ends and a player's right to medical privacy begins.

The controversy comes just one day after the Lions released an official statement explaining that Campbell continued to rest and recover at a local hospital.

"Jack Campbell continues to rest and recover at a local hospital. Preliminary indications are positive and out of respect for Jack and his family we will not share further information at this time."

Head coach Dan Campbell also declined to provide additional medical details during Monday's press conference, making it clear that the organization was respecting the family's wishes.

As we previously reported in our complete breakdown of Dan Campbell's comments, the coach visited Jack in the hospital Sunday night and spoke with his wife and parents.

Campbell described the family's outlook as positive while emphasizing that Jack's recovery remains the priority.

And as we covered following the Lions' encouraging hospital announcement, the team has deliberately limited the medical information being made public.

Henning is correct that fans care deeply about Jack Campbell. There is nothing unreasonable about wanting to know whether a player is recovering after such a frightening incident.

But wanting information and being entitled to that information are two different things.

The Lions have chosen to respect Campbell's privacy, and the reactions to Henning's post suggest that many fans believe everyone else should do the same.

For now, Jack Campbell's health and his family's wishes matter far more than satisfying the public's curiosity.

'Control Resonant' Becomes a Much-Needed Surprise Hit for Remedy

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Resonant is Remedy's fastest-selling game ever

Fans of Alan Wake and Control creator Remedy Entertainment can finally breathe a sigh of relief. After executive shake-ups and fiscal underperformance at the beloved studio, it seems that the team’s latest game is finding an audience. In a new report released on Tuesday, Remedy wrote that Control Resonant “received excellent player reception and became the fastest-selling Remedy game to date.” The company's third-quarter revenue more than tripled compared to last year, surging by 226%.

What’s more impressive is that the revenue spike includes only Resonant’s pre-orders and its first seven days of sales before the end of the company’s third quarter in late September. The game was released on Sept. 24.

Remedy’s new CEO, Jean-Charles Gaudechon, was quite happy with the game’s performance. “This is our first self-published game of this scale, built on our own Control IP. Praised by players and critics alike, it marks a defining milestone for the company.” Back on Oct. 15, Remedy announced that Resonant had already sold one million copies.

Gaudechon went on to highlight how the game was praised for “Remedy’s willingness to take creative risks and surprise its players.” He added that he was happy with the game’s technical performance. "Positive commentary over its scalability across a variety of PC hardware meant a larger audience could enjoy Control Resonant. This is a testament to our in-house Northlight game engine, which enables the distinctive look and feel that has come to define Remedy’s games.” While Resonant didn't receive unanimous critical praise, reviews were generally positive, albeit not as glowing as the studio's usual output.

Resonant was clearly a big swing for the company, as Gaudechon added that the game received the studio’s largest marketing investment in its history. The United States was its largest market, accounting for nearly half of sales. On PC, China and the U.K. ranked second and third after the U.S., while the U.K. and Canada were second and third for console sales.

Gaudechon expressed satisfaction that the game reached a wider audience. “Based on analytic data, close to 50% of players were new to Control,” he said. “Reaching new players was a key goal for this game, and these early results indicate a growing audience for the franchise and for Remedy.”

In June 2025, Remedy released the multiplayer game FBC: Firebreak, which received fairly tepid reviews and failed to find players. Remedy said the game didn’t meet sales expectations, prompting a profit warning that the company wouldn't hit its fiscal targets for the year. 12 days after the profit warning, former CEO Tero Virtala left his role. Remedy posted a €14.9 million operating loss for 2025.

On Feb. 9, 2026, the company announced that former Electronic Arts executive Gaudechon was the company’s new CEO. While there were concerns that the change would make Remedy resemble the much-hated EA, at least so far, that hasn’t happened. Hopefully, Control Resonant's success means the studio will remain a bastion of strange ideas in a game industry that's increasingly playing it safe.

Alan Wake 2 has been been rescued from The Dark Place.

Why Isn't Realty Income's Stock Taking Off After Strong Earnings Results?

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Realty Income delivered another solid quarter, but its results also help explain why the dividend stock hasn't taken off.

Realty Income (O -0.68%) continues to deliver the results investors expect from one of the world's largest real estate investment trusts (REITs). Its properties remain nearly fully occupied, its dividend is well covered, and its investment program continues to generate attractive returns.

However, despite another solid earnings report, the stock hasn't generated much excitement. The reason has less to do with the company's real estate portfolio and more to do with how quickly it can grow earnings per share.

Realty Income just keeps getting it done. The REIT grew its adjusted funds from operations (AFFO) by 3.7% in the third quarter to $1.12 per share. That put its dividend payout ratio at a conservative 72.6% of its AFFO, despite continued dividend growth. It has now delivered 5% AFFO per share growth through the first nine months of this year compared to the prior-year period. Its existing real estate portfolio continues to provide steadily rising cash flow. Occupancy was 98.8% at the end of the third quarter, while its rent recapture rate was 103.5% (meaning new leases on the same property were, on average, 3.5% above prior rates).

Meanwhile, the REIT continues to find attractive investment opportunities. It invested another $2.6 billion during the quarter at an initial cash yield of 6.8%. That includes properties acquired in its U.S. Core Fund and through joint ventures, such as its recently established strategic joint venture with KKR in Europe.

The company remains on pace to invest $10 billion this year. That supports its guidance to deliver $4.45-$4.46 per share of AFFO this year, a $0.01-per-share increase from its prior range. That's a more than 4% increase from last year despite headwinds from higher interest rates.

Despite its solid quarterly results, Realty Income's share price isn't soaring. That's likely due to three factors.

Realty Income's growth remains modest. AFFO per share grew just 3.7% despite a 10.3% increase in total revenue. Meanwhile, it only increased its full-year guidance by $0.01 per share at both ends of the range. Due to its large scale and net-lease focus, the REIT isn't growing as quickly as others in the industry.

It also continues to face some headwinds from interest rates. Higher rates increase interest expenses when refinancing existing debt and also drive up borrowing costs for new investments. That's a headwind for AFFO per share growth. Higher rates also weigh on the value of commercial real estate. Realty Income's share price has fallen almost 15% from its peak earlier this year, coinciding with the recent spike in the 10-year Treasury rate.

Given its size, Realty Income requires significant external capital to fund its investment program. Even highly attractive acquisitions can have a modest effect on AFFO per share when accounting for financing costs.

Realty Income isn't going to grow fast, which is why we shouldn't expect its stock to surge, even with a solid earnings report.

The thing with Realty Income is that investors don't need rapid growth to earn an attractive return. At its recent share price of around $57.50, the REIT offers a 5.6% yield at its current monthly dividend rate. It would only need to grow its AFFO per share by around a 4% annual rate to produce an average annualized total return approaching double digits. That assumes no change in its valuation multiple, which is currently a very reasonable 12.9 times.

Meanwhile, there's the potential for additional upside. Falling interest rates and incremental earnings from its expanding private capital platform could drive faster AFFO per share growth in the future. That could also drive some multiple expansion.

Realty Income doesn't need explosive earnings growth to deliver solid returns for investors. With a dividend yield around 5.6% and the potential to grow AFFO per share by more than 4% annually, the REIT could generate double-digit annualized total returns. Those returns likely won't come in one big post-earnings move but should arrive steadily as it continues to grow its earnings and pay its monthly dividend.

Matt DiLallo has positions in KKR and Realty Income. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends KKR and Realty Income. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

*Average returns of all recommendations since inception. Cost basis and return based on previous market day close.

Don't throw away your old AirPods — do this instead

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Instead of getting rid of your old AirPods, replace the battery and give them a new purpose.

AirPods are built to last, and they usually work for years before they stop functioning completely. You'll likely buy new AirPods before your old ones stop working, though they may no longer sound brand-new.

So what should you do with your old AirPods? Throwing them in the trash is something you certainly shouldn't do; Apple discourages this because of the lithium-ion batteries inside them. The good news is that, depending on the problem, Apple can fix your buds for a reasonable fee. Your old AirPods can become good backup earbuds, or even a gift for a friend or family member.

If battery performance is the only real problem, it's likely worth getting a replacement before buying new AirPods. Even if you've already bought a new pair, replacing the batteries in your old earbuds will make them better for someone else.

But unlike an iPhone or iPad, Apple won't open AirPods to replace their batteries. Instead, the company recycles your old AirPods and gives you new ones, charging you a lower price per earbud. For most AirPods, a battery replacement costs $49 per earbud, so $98 if you need to replace the battery in both. That's much cheaper than buying new AirPods Pro, for example, which cost $249. For the higher-end AirPods Max, a battery replacement costs $79, which is also much cheaper than paying $549 for new headphones.

If you have AppleCare, you may be eligible for a free battery replacement if any battery falls below 80 percent of its original capacity. And if any battery in your AirPods fails while they're still under warranty, Apple will replace them for free. Some independent repair shops can replace the battery inside AirPods, but this requires cutting them open and gluing them back together. You might end up saving some money, but they won't look as good as new — not to mention that they'll also lose their water and sweat resistance.

If you've lost one of your earbuds or the charging case, Apple offers replacements for them. Prices vary depending on the model; for the Pro line, a new earbud costs $89 and a new case costs $99. For standard AirPods, it's $69 to replace one bud and between $59 and $79 to replace the case.

It's thus usually worth it for the more expensive AirPods Pro, but not so much for the base AirPods. The latest AirPods 5 cost $129 from Apple, and it's not hard to find them for a little less at other stores.

Instead of tossing your old AirPods into a drawer, there are plenty of other ways to put them to good use. Earbuds that still hold a decent charge could be worth something. Apple doesn't offer trade-in credit for AirPods, but buyback services like Back Market will give you some money for them. Selling them directly on places like eBay or Facebook Marketplace usually pays more.

Or you can simply give them to a friend or family member, so they'll only have to pay for a battery replacement if needed. If you pass them on, be sure to clean the AirPods and remove them from your Apple account — new ear tips will be much appreciated.

Keeping them around is also suitable if they still work fine. Have your old AirPods ready as backup earbuds in case you lose your new ones, leave them somewhere or simply need another set when your usual pair runs out of battery. Some people prefer to use old AirPods as gym earbuds so their new ones don't get covered in sweat. If you like to sleep with your AirPods in, using your old ones can avoid draining the battery on the new ones. Or you might devote them for listening to your Roku at night via Bluetooth.

In case your old AirPods are truly dead and you don't want to spend money on them, Apple will recycle them for free. You don't even need to take them to an Apple Store, since Apple provides a prepaid shipping label in certain regions. You won't get paid for them, but at least your earbuds will be disposed of properly.
